It’s been making work feel really hard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I don’t know what exactly counts as “depressed,” you know? Like, I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer where I can point to it and go, yes, that’s what it is.\n\nI’ve been kind of not myself, I guess. But then I start overthinking the word “down,” because some days I can still talk to people and get through things, and then I wonder if I’m making too much of it. Sorry, that probably doesn’t answer it very directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really do the stuff I normally do. My guitar’s just been sitting in the corner, and every time I look at it I think I should pick it up, but then I just… don’t.\n\nI’ve been bailing on soccer too, and the shelter shifts, which I used to feel good about. And even when I try to do something, like cook something decent or go hiking, it feels kind of pointless, like I’m just doing the steps because I remember I’m supposed to like it.\n\nDoctor: For much of the day lately, have you been feeling sad or low?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “sad” feels like a really specific word, and I’m not sure I’d put it that way. Low, maybe? But then I think of low like when your phone battery is at 12% and you’re trying to decide if you should plug it in now or wait, which is probably not what you mean.\n\nI guess for a lot of the day I feel kind of… flattened out. Not exactly crying or anything. Just not up. Sorry, I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time these problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It kind of started about a month ago, around when the clinic lost some staff and everything got reshuffled.\n\nMy schedule moved into late-evening coverage, and there’s been a lot more crisis paperwork. Which sounds boring when I say it like that, but it really changed the whole rhythm of my days. Like, I used to know when I’d be home, and now I’m doing notes late and thinking about forms and coverage and… sorry, I’m getting off track. But yeah, that was around when this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, “on edge” maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it a lot. I’ve always had this thing where if a notification pops up, I kind of immediately look at it, which probably says more about phones than anxiety, right?\n\nLately I guess I’ve been… keyed up at moments, but it’s hard to separate from just being tired or distracted. I don’t think I’ve really been walking around panicky or anything. Sorry, I know that’s not a clean answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been weird. I’ll get home really tired, like I should just be able to pass out, but then I’m lying there for hours kind of replaying the whole day in my head.\n\nIt’s stupid stuff sometimes, like remembering one note I wrote or something someone said, and then somehow it turns into thinking about tomorrow, and then I look at the clock and it’s way later than I thought. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like, I used to know when I’d be home, and now I’m doing notes late and thinking about forms and coverage and… sorry, I’m getting off track. Like, “on edge” maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it a lot. I’ve always had this thing where if a notification pops up, I kind of immediately look at it, which probably says more about phones than anxiety, right?\n\nLately I guess I’ve been… keyed up at moments, but it’s hard to separate from just being tired or distracted. I don’t think I’ve really been walking around panicky or anything. Sorry, I know that’s not a clean answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been weird. I’ll get home really tired, like I should just be able to pass out, but then I’m lying there for hours kind of replaying the whole day in my head.\n\nIt’s stupid stuff sometimes, like remembering one note I wrote or something someone said, and then somehow it turns into thinking about tomorrow, and then I look at the clock and it’s way later than I thought. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “a lot more.” I mean… I worry, sure, but I feel like everyone worries when work gets messy. Like, even my roommate was saying the other day that he checks his email before bed, and I was like, see, that’s just normal now.\n\nBut for me, I’m not sure it’s exactly worry so much as my brain just keeps picking things up and putting them down again. Yeah… I think so. It’s been most days, for probably about a month now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone points it out, like “did you eat?” and then I’m like, oh, I guess I didn’t.

It’s weird because I used to be kind of routine about meals, or at least snacks. I had this whole thing where I’d buy the same sandwich from the place near work, and the guy there knew my order, which sounds depressing in a different way, but now I’ll just… forget. And my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.

I had this whole thing where I’d buy the same sandwich from the place near work, and the guy there knew my order, which sounds depressing in a different way, but now I’ll just… forget. Or not forget exactly, I just don’t feel like eating.\n\nAnd my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.\n\n[Last Question]\nLately, have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 20 / therapist / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Worthlessness_Guilt\nmanifestation: \"If I miss one detail in a note or forget to return a call right away, I keep telling myself I'm useless and that I'm letting the clients and everyone at the clinic down.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, I think that’s been happening a lot.

Like if I miss one little detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, my brain just goes straight to, “You’re useless, you’re letting everyone down.” And it’s not even always a huge mistake. Sometimes it’s just something small, like I forgot to add one thing and then I’m thinking about how the whole clinic probably has to clean up after me, which… I know that sounds dramatic.

It’s kind of like when you get one of those group emails about “please remember to do the thing,” and even if it wasn’t about you, you feel like it was absolutely about you. I do that constantly now. With clients, coworkers, everybody. I keep feeling like I’m failing them somehow.

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been most days, for probably about a month now.\n\nIt kind of started when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think they are stronger than just, like, “work is stressful.”

I mean, everyone misses things sometimes, right? Like a note detail or not calling someone back immediately. At the clinic there’s always some form that has six different boxes and one of them is apparently the important one—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, when I do it, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down. I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.”

It feels bigger than the actual thing I did. Like my reaction doesn’t match the mistake.

And even when I try to do something, like cook something decent or go hiking, it feels kind of pointless, like I’m just doing the steps because I remember I’m supposed to like it.\n\nDoctor: For much of the day lately, have you been feeling sad or low?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “sad” feels like a really specific word, and I’m not sure I’d put it that way. Not exactly crying or anything. Just not up. Sorry, I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time these problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It kind of started about a month ago, around when the clinic lost some staff and everything got reshuffled.\n\nMy schedule moved into late-evening coverage, and there’s been a lot more crisis paperwork. Which sounds boring when I say it like that, but it really changed the whole rhythm of my days. Like, I used to know when I’d be home, and now I’m doing notes late and thinking about forms and coverage and… sorry, I’m getting off track. But yeah, that was around when this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, “on edge” maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it a lot. I’ve always had this thing where if a notification pops up, I kind of immediately look at it, which probably says more about phones than anxiety, right?\n\nLately I guess I’ve been… keyed up at moments, but it’s hard to separate from just being tired or distracted. I don’t think I’ve really been walking around panicky or anything. Sorry, I know that’s not a clean answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been weird. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “a lot more.” I mean… I worry, sure, but I feel like everyone worries when work gets messy. Like, even my roommate was saying the other day that he checks his email before bed, and I was like, see, that’s just normal now.\n\nBut for me, I’m not sure it’s exactly worry so much as my brain just keeps picking things up and putting them down again. Like a movie version of it or something.\n\nI guess I have moments where I’m like… what am I even doing, but then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I never folded my laundry, or I’ll start thinking about this guy from high school who always seemed to have everything planned out. I don’t know why that popped into my head.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly. I don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone points it out, like “did you eat?” and then I’m like, oh, I guess I didn’t.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be kind of routine about meals, or at least snacks. Or not forget exactly, I just don’t feel like eating.\n\nAnd my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s been happening a lot.\n\nLike if I miss one little detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, my brain just goes straight to, “You’re useless, you’re letting everyone down.” And it’s not even always a huge mistake. Sometimes it’s just something small, like I forgot to add one thing and then I’m thinking about how the whole clinic probably has to clean up after me, which… I know that sounds dramatic.\n\nIt’s kind of like when you get one of those group emails about “please remember to do the thing,” and even if it wasn’t about you, you feel like it was absolutely about you. I do that constantly now. With clients, coworkers, everybody. I keep feeling like I’m failing them somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you’d expect just from the work changes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they are stronger than just, like, “work is stressful.”\n\nI mean, everyone misses things sometimes, right? Like a note detail or not calling someone back immediately. At the clinic there’s always some form that has six different boxes and one of them is apparently the important one—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, when I do it, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down. I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.”\n\nIt feels bigger than the actual thing I did. Like my reaction doesn’t match the mistake.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling down or depressed most of the day nearly every day lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 16\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."

I… I don’t know how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. Like, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that. It’s more like… I wake up and there’s this heaviness, and then I go do what I’m supposed to do, and sometimes I get distracted by something random, like a client’s scheduling form or whether I left my mug in the staff room again.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I came in because my head just hasn’t been working right lately. Like at work, I’ll be sitting with a client and suddenly realize I’ve lost the thread of what they were saying, even though I’m trying to listen. And then I’ll look at a chart note and read the same line over and over, like when you reread a menu even though you’re not hungry—sorry, that’s not really related.\n\nIt’s just… simple decisions I used to make without thinking, I kind of freeze on now. It’s been making work feel really hard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I don’t know what exactly counts as “depressed,” you know? Like, I wouldn’t say I have a clean answer where I can point to it and go, yes, that’s what it is.\n\nI’ve been kind of not myself, I guess. But then I start overthinking the word “down,” because some days I can still talk to people and get through things, and then I wonder if I’m making too much of it. Sorry, that probably doesn’t answer it very directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really do the stuff I normally do. My guitar’s just been sitting in the corner, and every time I look at it I think I should pick it up, but then I just… don’t.\n\nI’ve been bailing on soccer too, and the shelter shifts, which I used to feel good about. And even when I try to do something, like cook something decent or go hiking, it feels kind of pointless, like I’m just doing the steps because I remember I’m supposed to like it.\n\nDoctor: For much of the day lately, have you been feeling sad or low?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “sad” feels like a really specific word, and I’m not sure I’d put it that way. Low, maybe? But then I think of low like when your phone battery is at 12% and you’re trying to decide if you should plug it in now or wait, which is probably not what you mean.\n\nI guess for a lot of the day I feel kind of… flattened out. Not exactly crying or anything. Just not up. Sorry, I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time these problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It kind of started about a month ago, around when the clinic lost some staff and everything got reshuffled.\n\nMy schedule moved into late-evening coverage, and there’s been a lot more crisis paperwork. Which sounds boring when I say it like that, but it really changed the whole rhythm of my days. Like, I used to know when I’d be home, and now I’m doing notes late and thinking about forms and coverage and… sorry, I’m getting off track. But yeah, that was around when this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, “on edge” maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it a lot. I’ve always had this thing where if a notification pops up, I kind of immediately look at it, which probably says more about phones than anxiety, right?\n\nLately I guess I’ve been… keyed up at moments, but it’s hard to separate from just being tired or distracted. I don’t think I’ve really been walking around panicky or anything. Sorry, I know that’s not a clean answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been weird. I’ll get home really tired, like I should just be able to pass out, but then I’m lying there for hours kind of replaying the whole day in my head.\n\nIt’s stupid stuff sometimes, like remembering one note I wrote or something someone said, and then somehow it turns into thinking about tomorrow, and then I look at the clock and it’s way later than I thought. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “a lot more.” I mean… I worry, sure, but I feel like everyone worries when work gets messy. Like, even my roommate was saying the other day that he checks his email before bed, and I was like, see, that’s just normal now.\n\nBut for me, I’m not sure it’s exactly worry so much as my brain just keeps picking things up and putting them down again. Like I’ll think about one thing, then something totally unrelated, and then I’m like, why am I thinking about grocery bags right now? Sorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed it as some big increase, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t really noticed myself talking slower. Sometimes I’ll pause because I’m trying to think of the right word, but I feel like I’ve always done that, like in school presentations and stuff.\n\nMoving slower… maybe if I’m tired, but not in a way where someone’s said anything to me. I don’t know. I guess I haven’t been paying attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, “hopeless” sounds really big, you know? Like a movie version of it or something.\n\nI guess I have moments where I’m like… what am I even doing, but then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I never folded my laundry, or I’ll start thinking about this guy from high school who always seemed to have everything planned out. I don’t know why that popped into my head.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly. Maybe I just haven’t really thought about that word.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t have like a plan or anything. I’m not sitting there figuring out how to hurt myself.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve had thoughts like… if I just didn’t wake up, maybe that would be easier. And then I get freaked out because I know that’s not just some random thought, you know? Like, I’ll be brushing my teeth or staring at my phone, and it’ll just kind of show up, and then I’m thinking about how people say stuff like that and it means something bad, and then I feel weird for even thinking it.\n\nSo… yeah. Not a plan, but the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been most days, for probably about a month now.\n\nIt kind of started when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. And then the crisis paperwork got heavier too, which… I know sounds like just a work thing, but it’s sort of when I noticed everything started feeling like this. Like before that I was busy, but this has felt different. So, yeah, definitely more than two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Or not forget exactly, I just don’t feel like eating.\n\nAnd my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s been happening a lot.\n\nLike if I miss one little detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, my brain just goes straight to, “You’re useless, you’re letting everyone down.” And it’s not even always a huge mistake. I mean, I haven’t really noticed myself talking slower. I don’t know. I guess I haven’t been paying attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, “hopeless” sounds really big, you know? Like a movie version of it or something.\n\nI guess I have moments where I’m like… what am I even doing, but then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I never folded my laundry, or I’ll start thinking about this guy from high school who always seemed to have everything planned out. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “a lot more.” I mean… I worry, sure, but I feel like everyone worries when work gets messy. Like, even my roommate was saying the other day that he checks his email before bed, and I was like, see, that’s just normal now.\n\nBut for me, I’m not sure it’s exactly worry so much as my brain just keeps picking things up and putting them down again. Like I’ll think about one thing, then something totally unrelated, and then I’m like, why am I thinking about grocery bags right now? Sorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed it as some big increase, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t really noticed myself talking slower. Sometimes I’ll pause because I’m trying to think of the right word, but I feel like I’ve always done that, like in school presentations and stuff.\n\nMoving slower… maybe if I’m tired, but not in a way where someone’s said anything to me. I don’t know. I guess I haven’t been paying attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, “hopeless” sounds really big, you know? Like a movie version of it or something.\n\nI guess I have moments where I’m like… what am I even doing, but then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I never folded my laundry, or I’ll start thinking about this guy from high school who always seemed to have everything planned out. I don’t know why that popped into my head.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly. Maybe I just haven’t really thought about that word.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t have like a plan or anything. I’m not sitting there figuring out how to hurt myself.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve had thoughts like… if I just didn’t wake up, maybe that would be easier. And then I get freaked out because I know that’s not just some random thought, you know? Like, I’ll be brushing my teeth or staring at my phone, and it’ll just kind of show up, and then I’m thinking about how people say stuff like that and it means something bad, and then I feel weird for even thinking it.\n\nSo… yeah. Not a plan, but the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been most days, for probably about a month now.\n\nIt kind of started when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. And then the crisis paperwork got heavier too, which… I know sounds like just a work thing, but it’s sort of when I noticed everything started feeling like this. Like before that I was busy, but this has felt different. So, yeah, definitely more than two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone points it out, like “did you eat?” and then I’m like, oh, I guess I didn’t.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be kind of routine about meals, or at least snacks. I had this whole thing where I’d buy the same sandwich from the place near work, and the guy there knew my order, which sounds depressing in a different way, but now I’ll just… forget. Or not forget exactly, I just don’t feel like eating.\n\nAnd my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s been happening a lot.\n\nLike if I miss one little detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, my brain just goes straight to, “You’re useless, you’re letting everyone down.” And it’s not even always a huge mistake. Sometimes it’s just something small, like I forgot to add one thing and then I’m thinking about how the whole clinic probably has to clean up after me, which… I know that sounds dramatic.\n\nIt’s kind of like when you get one of those group emails about “please remember to do the thing,” and even if it wasn’t about you, you feel like it was absolutely about you. I do that constantly now. With clients, coworkers, everybody. I keep feeling like I’m failing them somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you’d expect just from the work changes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they are stronger than just, like, “work is stressful.”\n\nI mean, everyone misses things sometimes, right? Like a note detail or not calling someone back immediately. At the clinic there’s always some form that has six different boxes and one of them is apparently the important one—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, when I do it, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down. I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.”\n\nIt feels bigger than the actual thing I did. Like my reaction doesn’t match the mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day nearly every day lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. Like, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that. It’s more like… I wake up and there’s this heaviness, and then I go do what I’m supposed to do, and sometimes I get distracted by something random, like a client’s scheduling form or whether I left my mug in the staff room again.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe? I’m not sure. I feel low a lot, but I don’t know if it’s every day in the exact way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I’ve definitely felt down a lot this past month, but “nearly every day” makes me feel like I should be able to count it, and I haven’t really been keeping track.\n\nSome days I get through things and I’m just kind of… flat, I guess. Other days I don’t notice it as much until later, like when I’m sitting there after work and realize I’ve been in that low mood for hours. But I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure what you mean by nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: In the past month, have you felt low or down on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I think… probably? But I’m still kind of hesitating because “more days than not” sounds simple, and then my brain starts trying to make it exact.\n\nLike, I remember a bunch of days feeling low, especially when I’m just going through the day and everything feels heavier than it should. But I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything. I used to do that for workouts for like two weeks, actually, and then I forgot, so I’m not great at tracking stuff.\n\nSo… I guess my best answer is that it feels like it’s been happening a lot, maybe more days than not, but I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: During the past month, have you felt low or down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure I can say “nearly every day” with confidence. It’s been a lot, yeah, like it kind of follows me around, but then I get caught up in things and I don’t always notice it the same way.\n\nI know that sounds vague. I guess I’m stuck on the wording. Like, if I had a calendar, maybe I could tell you, but I don’t. My week kind of blends together sometimes—work stuff, laundry, forgetting to buy toothpaste, that sort of thing. So… I’ve felt low many days, but I don’t know if I can honestly say nearly every day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I used to do that for workouts for like two weeks, actually, and then I forgot, so I’m not great at tracking stuff.\n\nSo… I guess my best answer is that it feels like it’s been happening a lot, maybe more days than not, but I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: During the past month, have you felt low or down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure I can say “nearly every day” with confidence. It’s been a lot, yeah, like it kind of follows me around, but then I get caught up in things and I don’t always notice it the same way.\n\nI know that sounds vague. I guess I’m stuck on the wording. Like, if I had a calendar, maybe I could tell you, but I don’t. My week kind of blends together sometimes—work stuff, laundry, forgetting to buy toothpaste, that sort of thing. So… I’ve felt low many days, but I don’t know if I can honestly say nearly every day.\n\n[Last Question]\nIf the staffing and schedule changes at work had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 20 / therapist / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about a month, since the clinic lost staff and my schedule shifted into late-evening coverage and heavier crisis paperwork.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… probably not in the same way, no. It really feels like it started around when the clinic lost staff and my schedule got shifted into late-evening coverage, plus all the extra crisis paperwork.

It’s been, like, about a month now. Which is weird because at first I kept thinking, “Okay, this is just a rough week,” and then suddenly it was every week. I was even joking with someone that my laundry schedule is more predictable than my actual work schedule now, which is… not that funny, I guess.

So yeah, I think the work changes are a big part of why I’ve been feeling this way.

Like at work, I’ll be sitting with a client and suddenly realize I’ve lost the thread of what they were saying, even though I’m trying to listen. And then I’ll look at a chart note and read the same line over and over, like when you reread a menu even though you’re not hungry—sorry, that’s not really related.\n\nIt’s just… simple decisions I used to make without thinking, I kind of freeze on now. It’s been making work feel really hard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I don’t know what exactly counts as “depressed,” you know? But then I start overthinking the word “down,” because some days I can still talk to people and get through things, and then I wonder if I’m making too much of it. Sorry, that probably doesn’t answer it very directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t really do the stuff I normally do. Not exactly crying or anything. Just not up. Sorry, I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time these problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Which sounds boring when I say it like that, but it really changed the whole rhythm of my days. Like, I used to know when I’d be home, and now I’m doing notes late and thinking about forms and coverage and… sorry, I’m getting off track. But yeah, that was around when this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, “on edge” maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it a lot. I’ve always had this thing where if a notification pops up, I kind of immediately look at it, which probably says more about phones than anxiety, right?\n\nLately I guess I’ve been… keyed up at moments, but it’s hard to separate from just being tired or distracted. I don’t think I’ve really been walking around panicky or anything. Sorry, I know that’s not a clean answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your sleep has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been weird. I’ll get home really tired, like I should just be able to pass out, but then I’m lying there for hours kind of replaying the whole day in my head.\n\nIt’s stupid stuff sometimes, like remembering one note I wrote or something someone said, and then somehow it turns into thinking about tomorrow, and then I look at the clock and it’s way later than I thought. And even when I do fall asleep, I’ve been waking up before my alarm feeling like I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “a lot more.” I mean… I worry, sure, but I feel like everyone worries when work gets messy. Like, even my roommate was saying the other day that he checks his email before bed, and I was like, see, that’s just normal now.\n\nBut for me, I’m not sure it’s exactly worry so much as my brain just keeps picking things up and putting them down again. Like I’ll think about one thing, then something totally unrelated, and then I’m like, why am I thinking about grocery bags right now? Sorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed it as some big increase, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t really noticed myself talking slower. I don’t know. I guess I haven’t been paying attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, “hopeless” sounds really big, you know? Like a movie version of it or something.\n\nI guess I have moments where I’m like… what am I even doing, but then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I never folded my laundry, or I’ll start thinking about this guy from high school who always seemed to have everything planned out. I don’t know why that popped into my head.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly. Maybe I just haven’t really thought about that word.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t have like a plan or anything. I’m not sitting there figuring out how to hurt myself.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve had thoughts like… if I just didn’t wake up, maybe that would be easier. And then I get freaked out because I know that’s not just some random thought, you know? Like, I’ll be brushing my teeth or staring at my phone, and it’ll just kind of show up, and then I’m thinking about how people say stuff like that and it means something bad, and then I feel weird for even thinking it.\n\nSo… yeah. Not a plan, but the thoughts are there, and they scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been most days, for probably about a month now.\n\nIt kind of started when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. And then the crisis paperwork got heavier too, which… I know sounds like just a work thing, but it’s sort of when I noticed everything started feeling like this. Like before that I was busy, but this has felt different. So, yeah, definitely more than two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone points it out, like “did you eat?” and then I’m like, oh, I guess I didn’t.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be kind of routine about meals, or at least snacks. I had this whole thing where I’d buy the same sandwich from the place near work, and the guy there knew my order, which sounds depressing in a different way, but now I’ll just… forget. Or not forget exactly, I just don’t feel like eating.\n\nAnd my pants have been looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s been happening a lot.\n\nLike if I miss one little detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, my brain just goes straight to, “You’re useless, you’re letting everyone down.” And it’s not even always a huge mistake. Sometimes it’s just something small, like I forgot to add one thing and then I’m thinking about how the whole clinic probably has to clean up after me, which… I know that sounds dramatic.\n\nIt’s kind of like when you get one of those group emails about “please remember to do the thing,” and even if it wasn’t about you, you feel like it was absolutely about you. I do that constantly now. With clients, coworkers, everybody. I keep feeling like I’m failing them somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you’d expect just from the work changes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they are stronger than just, like, “work is stressful.”\n\nI mean, everyone misses things sometimes, right? Like a note detail or not calling someone back immediately. At the clinic there’s always some form that has six different boxes and one of them is apparently the important one—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, when I do it, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down. I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.”\n\nIt feels bigger than the actual thing I did. Like my reaction doesn’t match the mistake.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day nearly every day lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. Like, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that. It’s more like… I wake up and there’s this heaviness, and then I go do what I’m supposed to do, and sometimes I get distracted by something random, like a client’s scheduling form or whether I left my mug in the staff room again.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe? I’m not sure. Like, I’ve definitely felt down a lot this past month, but “nearly every day” makes me feel like I should be able to count it, and I haven’t really been keeping track.\n\nSome days I get through things and I’m just kind of… flat, I guess. So… I’ve felt low many days, but I don’t know if I can honestly say nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: If the staffing and schedule changes at work had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I mean… probably not in the same way, no.
